stays small, which means it cannot be activated and has only weak alignment. (c) A neuron can only be well aligned with a small number of features, not with many at the same time. (d) The projection of a neuron weight on non-feature directions is very small, which means the neuron does not learn the non-feature directions. (e) The size of each neuron weight is controlled by its bias, so the weight does not grow without limit.

Lemma D.1
.

For all iterations
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
, the neurons
i\in[m]
satisfy the following properties:

(a) For
j\in[d]
, if
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, then

\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\geq\sqrt{1+\gamma}\,\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}.

(66)

(b) For
j\in[d]
, if
i\notin\mathcal{M}_{j}
, then

\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{1-\gamma}\,\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)},

(67)

and furthermore,

\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\right).

(68)

(c) For each
i\in[m]
, there are at most
\mathcal{O}(2^{-\sqrt{\log d}}d)
many
j\in[d]
such that

\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\;\geq\;\frac{(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\sqrt{\log d}}.

(69)

(d) For each
i\in[m]
, and for all
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
,

\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d_{1}}}\right).

(70)

(e) For all
i\in[m]
,

\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{d(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\log d}.

(71)

Lemma D.2
.

For each
i\in[m]
, define

\Lambda_{i}:=\left\{j\in[d]:|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq\tfrac{\sigma_{0}}{d}\right\}\subseteq[d].

(72)

Then

|\Lambda_{i}|=O\!\left(\tfrac{d}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right).

(73)

D.3
Proof of Theorem D.1

Proof of Theorem
D.1
:

We follow similar analysis as in the proof of Lemma
D.1
. In order to prove (a)-(c), we have to discuss the two substages of the learning process below.

When all
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq(1+\frac{\varepsilon_{\min}}{\varepsilon_{\max}})\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}
:
From similar analysis in the proof of Lemma
D.1
, the iteration complexity for a neuron
i\in[m]
to reach

\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\geq(1+\frac{\varepsilon_{\min}}{\varepsilon_{\max}})\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}
is no smaller than

T^{\prime}_{i,1}:=\max\left\{T_{1}+\Omega\left(\frac{d\log d}{\epsilon_{\max}\eta\log\log d}\right),T_{2}\right\}.

(74)

When some
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\geq(1+\frac{\varepsilon_{j}}{\varepsilon_{\max}})\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}
.

We first prove Theorem
D.1
(a). In the first stage, for
j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d],j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d],j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{O(\eta)}{d\,\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)^{T_{2}}+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}^{2}}{d^{3/2}}\right)

(75)

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1)(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})+o(1)(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2})\left\|\bm{MM}^{\top}\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where we used the fact that
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\bm{MM}^{\top}\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}
.

For
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})},\bm{M}^{\perp}_{j}\rangle^{2}

(76)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}^{\perp}_{j}\rangle^{2}+O\left(\frac{\eta(T^{\prime}_{i,1}-T_{1})}{d}\right)e^{-\Omega(\log^{1/4}d)}\max_{t^{\prime}\in[T_{1},T^{\prime}_{i,1}]}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t^{\prime})}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(1+o\big(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\big)\Big)\left\|\bm{M}^{\perp}(\bm{M}^{\perp})^{\top}\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Typically, if
i\in\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j},
there exists
t\leq T_{2}
such that
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\geq(1+\frac{\varepsilon_{j}}{\varepsilon_{\max}})\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{2})}\|_{2}
, as we have argued in the proof of Lemma
D.1
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})}\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{j\in[d],j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}-\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})},\bm{M}^{\perp}_{j}\rangle^{2}

(77)

\displaystyle\geq(1+\frac{\varepsilon_{j}}{\varepsilon_{\max}})^{2}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}^{2}-(1+o(1)(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})+o(1)(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2})\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{j}}{\varepsilon_{\max}}\cdot(2-o(1))\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}^{2},

which proves the claim.

In the second stage, if
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, then from similar calculations as above, we can prove by induction that starting from
t=T^{\prime}_{i,1}
, it holds:

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|

\displaystyle\geq|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\left(1+\Omega\left(\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta\log\log d}{d}\right)\right)

(78)

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\left(1+\Omega\left(\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta\log\log d}{d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d],j^{\prime}\neq j}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d],j^{\prime}\neq j}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}}\rangle^{2}\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{O(\eta)}{d\,\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}^{\perp}_{j}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}^{\perp}_{j}\rangle^{2}\left(1+\frac{O(\eta)}{d\,\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)^{2},

which implies

|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\geq|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\cdot\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}\geq(1-o(1))\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}.

(79)

Next, we prove Theorem
D.1
(b). In the first stage, the bias growth is large, i.e.,

\displaystyle\bm{b}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})}

\displaystyle\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}(1+\frac{\eta}{d})^{T^{\prime}_{i,1}-T_{1}}\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\cdot\operatorname{polylog}(d)

(80)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}{\sqrt{d}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}\geq\frac{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}{\sqrt{d}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})}\|_{2}.

In the second stage, the bias is large consistently, i.e.,

\bm{b}_{i}^{(t+1)}\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\cdot\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}\geq\frac{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}{\sqrt{d}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T^{\prime}_{i,1})}\|_{2}.

(81)

Finally, we prove Theorem
D.1
(c): Assuming
\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle>0
(the opposite case is similar), from
t=T_{1}
, for
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\right)\pm O\left(\frac{\eta|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|}{d\,\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)

(82)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(1-\frac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(1-\frac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)\right)^{t+1-T_{1}}.

which implies that after certain iteration
t=T_{1}+T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}=\Theta\left(\frac{d}{\eta}\right)
, we shall have

|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1}+T^{\prime})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\geq\log\log d\cdot|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\cdot\log\log d.

(83)

However, at iteration

t=T_{1}+\Theta\left(\frac{d}{\eta}\right)
, we can see from previous analysis that
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq(1+o(1))\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}
, so the bias growth can be bounded as

\displaystyle\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\left(1+\frac{\eta}{d}\right)^{\Theta(\frac{d}{\eta})}\cdot\max\left\{\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}},1\right\}

(84)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}\left(1+\frac{\eta}{d}\cdot{\Theta(\frac{d}{\eta})}\right)\cdot\max\left\{(1+o(1)),1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq O(\bm{b}_{i}^{(T_{1})}).

Now from our initialization properties in Lemma
D.2
, we have that
\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(0)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\leq O(\sigma_{0}^{2}\log d)
for all
i\in[m]
. Thus via similar arguments, we also have

|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(0)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(1\pm\frac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)\right)^{t}.

(85)

holds for all
i^{\prime}\in[m]
. Now it is easy to see that for
t\leq T_{2}=T_{1}+\Theta\left(\frac{d\tau\log d}{\eta\log\log d}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|}{|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|}

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(1-\frac{\eta}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)\right)^{t-T_{1}}}{|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(T_{1})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta C_{z}\log\log d}{d}+\frac{\eta}{d\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)^{t-T_{1}}}

(86)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-O\left(\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta\log\log d}{d\operatorname{polylog}(d))}\right)\right)^{t-T_{1}}\geq\Omega(1).

Thus, the last claim is proved.
∎

D.4
Proof of Lemma 3.2

Lemma
3.2
can be viewed as an informal version of Theorem
D.1
.
In particular, part (a) of Lemma
3.2
corresponds to (
77
) and Lemma
B.2
(c), while part (b) of Lemma
3.2
corresponds to another formulation of Theorem
D.1
(c).

Appendix E
Theorem E.1

E.1
Theorem E.1

At the final stage, we show that sparse activation of neurons naturally leads to convergence toward sparse solutions, thereby guaranteeing sparse representations. For all
t\geq T_{2}
:

Theorem E.1
.

For all iterations
t
, the neurons
i\in[m]
satisfy the following properties:

(a) For
j\in[d]
, if
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, then

\bigl|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bigr|\;\geq\;\Omega(1)\,\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(87)

(b) For
i\in[m]
, we have

\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\;\leq\;O(1).

(88)

(c) For each
j\in[d]
,

\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}=\Theta((\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2}\tau\log^{3}d).

(89)

(d) Let
j\in[d]
and
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, then there exists
C=\Theta(1)
such that

\bigl|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bigr|\;\geq\;C\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\bigl|\langle w_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bigr|.

(90)

(e) For
i\notin\mathcal{M}_{j}
, it holds

\bigl|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bigr|\;\leq\;O\!\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{d}\,\Xi^{5}_{2}}\right)\,\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(91)

(f) For any
i\in[m]
and any
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
, it holds

\bigl|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\bigr|\;\leq\;O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{d_{1}}\,\Xi^{5}_{2}}\right)\,\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(92)

(g) For all
i\in[m]
, the bias satisfies

\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\;\geq\;\tfrac{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}{\sqrt{d}}\,\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(93)

E.2
Useful Lemmas

When all the conditions in Theorem
E.1
hold for some iteration
t\geq T_{2}
, we have the following fact, which is a simple corollary of Lemma
E.9
.

Lemma E.1
.

For any
i\in[m]
, we denote
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\{j\in[d]:i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\}
. Suppose Theorem
E.1
holds at iteration
t\geq T_{2}
, then with high probability over
x\in\mathcal{D}_{x}
:

\max_{x\in\{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\}}\bm{1}_{h_{i,t}(x)\neq 0}\;\leq\;\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\bm{1}_{|\hat{z}_{p,j}|\neq 0},

(94)

which implies that

\max_{x\in\{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\}\cup\mathfrak{N}}\Pr\!\big(h_{i,t}(x)\neq 0\big)\;\leq\;O\!\left(\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right).

(95)

Now for the simplicity of calculations, we define the following notations which are used through out this section

Definition E.1

(Expansion of gradient)
.

For each
i\in[m],\ j\in[d]
, we expand
\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}L(f_{t}),\bm{M}_{j}\rangle
as

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}L(f_{t}),\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

(96)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left((\ell_{p,t}^{\prime}-1)h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})+\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\right)\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\langle\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right],

and

\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}L(f_{t}),\bm{M}_{j}\rangle=\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}+\Phi_{i,j}^{(t)}+\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)},

(97)

where the
\Psi^{(t)}
,
\Phi^{(t)}
,
\mathcal{E}^{(t)}
are defined as follows.
For each

\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}=\frac{1}{L}\left(\sum_{j}\bm{M}_{j}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}\right)\sim\mathcal{D}_{\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}},\quad\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}=\frac{1}{L}\left(\sum_{j}\bm{M}_{j}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{+}\right)\sim\mathcal{D}_{\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}},

(98)

we write

\displaystyle\psi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{Y}_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{L}\big[\left(\frac{1}{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\bm{1}_{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle>\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}}

(99)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}+\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\bm{1}_{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle<-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}}\big],

\phi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{Y}_{n})=\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z}_{Y}^{(s)\setminus j}\rangle\bm{1}_{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle>\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}}-\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z}_{Y}^{(s)\setminus j}\rangle\bm{1}_{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle<-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}}.

(100)

Now we define

\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left((\ell_{p,t}^{\prime}-1)\cdot\psi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{Y}_{n})+\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}\cdot\psi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\right)\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\right],

(101)

\Phi_{i,j}^{(t)}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left((\ell_{p,t}^{\prime}-1)\cdot\phi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{Y}_{n})+\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}\cdot\phi_{i,j}^{(t)}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\right)\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\right],

(102)

\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left((\ell_{p,t}^{\prime}-1)\cdot h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})+\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}\cdot h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\right)\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\langle\bm{M}_{j},\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle\right].

(103)

Moreover, for
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
, we can similarly define

\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)},\ \Phi_{i,j}^{(t)}\equiv 0,

(104)

\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left((\ell_{p,t}^{\prime}-1)\cdot h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})+\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}\cdot h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\right)\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\langle\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp},\ \tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle\right].

(105)

Equipped with the above definition, we are ready to characterize the training process at the final stage.

Lemma E.2

(Lower bound for
\Psi^{(t)}_{1}
)
.

Suppose Theorem
E.1
holds at iteration
t
.
For
j\in[d]
and
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, there exists
G_{1}=\Theta(1)
such that if

\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}:=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\right)^{2}\;\leq\;\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}G_{1}\tau\log d,

(106)

then we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}\cdot\mathrm{sign}\!\left(\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\right)

(107)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{r=1}^{L}\big|\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\big|\right]}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\left(1-O\!\left(\frac{1}{\Xi^{3}_{2}}\right)\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{L}\big|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\big|\right).

Lemma E.3

(Upper bound for
\Psi^{(t)}_{i,j}
)
.

Let
j\in[d]
and
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
.
Suppose Theorem
E.1
holds at iteration
t
, then there exists a constant
G_{2}=\Theta(1)
such that if

\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}:=\sum_{j:\,i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\right)^{2}\;\geq\;\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}G_{2}\tau\log d,

(108)

we have

\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}\sum_{s=1}^{L}\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\right|.

(109)

Similarly, for
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
, we have

\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}\sum_{s=1}^{L}\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\right|+O\!\left(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}.

(110)

Lemma E.4
.

At iteration
t\geq T_{2}
, let
j\in[d]
and
i\in[m]
. Suppose Theorem
E.1
holds at
t
.
Then for each
j\in[d_{1}]
, we have

\left|\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}\right|\;\leq\;O\!\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}\,\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{d^{2}\tau}\right)\cdot\max_{i^{\prime}\in[m]}\left(\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\right).

(111)

Lemma E.5

(Reduction of
\Phi^{(t)}
to the bounds of
\Psi^{(t)}
)
.

Let
j\in[d]
and
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
Suppose Theorem
E.1
holds for all iterations before
t\in\left[\tfrac{d^{1.01}}{\eta},\tfrac{d^{1.99}}{\eta}\right]
and after
T_{2}
.
Also suppose that for all
l\in[d]
, we have

\mathfrak{F}^{(t^{\prime})}_{l}=\Omega\!\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}\tau\log d\right)\quad\text{at some }t^{\prime}=\Theta(T_{2}).

(112)

Then the following bounds hold:

For iteration
t\in\left[\tfrac{d^{1.01}}{\eta},\tfrac{d^{1.495}}{\eta}\right]
,

\Phi^{(t)}_{i,j}\;\leq\;\widetilde{O}\!\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\cdot\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{3/2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(113)

For iteration
t\in\left[\tfrac{d^{1.495}}{\eta},\tfrac{d^{1.99}}{\eta}\right]
,

\Phi^{(t)}_{i,j}\;\leq\;\widetilde{O}\!\left(\frac{1}{d^{1.98}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(114)

Definition E.2

(Optimal Learner)
.

We define a learner network that we deem as the optimal feature map for this task. Let
\kappa>0
, we define
\theta^{\star}:=\{\theta_{i}^{\star}\}_{i\in[m]}
as follows:

\theta_{i}^{\star}=\begin{cases}\dfrac{\sqrt{\tau}\,\kappa}{|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}|}\,\bm{M}_{j}\cdot\operatorname{sign}\!\bigl(\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{2})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bigr),&\text{if }i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star},\\[8.61108pt]
0,&\text{if }i\notin\bigcup_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}.\end{cases}

(115)

Furthermore, we define the optimal feature map
f_{t}^{\star}
as follows.
For
i\in[m]
, the
i
-th neuron of
f_{t,\theta}
given weight
\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
is

f_{t,\theta,i}(\bm{X}_{n})\;=\;\sum_{r=1}^{L}\left[\bigl(\langle\theta_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle-\bm{b}_{i}\bigr)\bm{1}_{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle\geq\bm{b}_{i}}-\bigl(-\langle\theta_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle-\bm{b}_{i}\bigr)\bm{1}_{-\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\right].

(116)

Finally, we write
f_{t,\theta}
as the concatenation

f_{t,\theta}(\cdot)\;=\;\bigl(f_{t,\theta,1}(\cdot),\ldots,f_{t,\theta,m}(\cdot)\bigr)^{\top}.

(117)

Lemma E.6

(Optimality)
.

Let
\{\theta_{i}^{\star}\}_{i\in[m]}
and
f_{t,\theta}
be defined as in Definition
E.1
. When Theorem
E.1
, define the pseudo loss function

\widetilde{L}(f_{t,\theta^{\star}},f_{t}):=\mathbb{E}\left[-\tau\log\left(\frac{e^{\langle f_{t,\theta^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\rangle/\tau}}{\sum_{\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{B}}e^{\langle f_{t,\theta^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{X})\rangle/\tau}}\right)\right].

(118)

Then by choosing
\kappa=\Theta(\Xi_{2})
, and assuming

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}}|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\;\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{\Xi_{2}}\right),

(119)

we obtain the following loss guarantee:

\widetilde{L}(f_{t,\theta^{\star}},f_{t})\;\leq\;O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\log d}\right).

(120)

Lemma E.7

(Pre-activation size I)
.

Let
\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}=\frac{1}{L}\left(\bm{M}\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)}_{n}+\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\right)\sim\mathcal{D}_{\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}},\quad\bm{w}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
.
Define
\bm{z}^{(r)\setminus j}_{X}=\frac{1}{L}\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j,\,j^{\prime}\in[d]}\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}}\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)}_{n,j^{\prime}}+\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\right).
Then the following results hold:

(a) Naive Chebyshev bound: For any
\lambda>0
,

\Pr_{\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)\setminus j}_{n},\,\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}}\!\left(\left(\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{z}^{(r)\setminus j}_{X}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)}_{n,j}\right)^{2}>\tfrac{\lambda\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\sqrt{\log d}}{d}\right)\leq O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\right).

(121)

The same tail bound applies to
\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle
,
\langle\bm{w}_{i},\tfrac{\bm{z}_{Y}^{(s)}-\bm{z}_{X}^{(r)}}{2}\rangle
, and
\langle\bm{w}_{i},\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle
.

(b) High probability bound for sparse signal:

\Pr\!\left(\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle^{2}>\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\max_{j\in[d]}\|\bm{M}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\log^{4}d\right)\lesssim e^{-\Omega(\log^{2}d)}.

(122)

(c) High probability bound for dense signal:
Let
Z=\langle\bm{w}_{i},\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle
. Then

\Pr\!\left(\bm{z}^{2}\geq\tfrac{\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\log^{4}d}{d}\right)\lesssim e^{-\Omega(\log^{2}d)}.

(123)

Lemma E.8

(Pre-activation size II)
.

Suppose the following conditions hold:

\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\geq\Omega\!\big((\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}\big)\quad\text{for at most }O(1)\text{ indices }j\in[d],

(124)

\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\geq\Omega\!\left(\frac{(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\sqrt{\log d}}\right)\quad\text{for at most }O\!\big(e^{-\Omega(\sqrt{\log d})}d\big)\text{ indices }j\in[d],

(125)

\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O\!\left(\frac{d(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\log d}\right).

(126)

Then, for any
\lambda\geq 0.0001
,

\Pr\!\left(\big|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle\big|\geq\lambda\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\lesssim e^{-\Omega(\log^{1/4}d)},

(127)

and

\Pr\!\left(\Big|\Big\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\tfrac{\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}+\bm{z}^{(s)}_{X}}{2}\Big\rangle\Big|\geq\lambda\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\lesssim e^{-\Omega(\log^{1/4}d)}.

(128)

Lemma E.9

(Pre-activation size III)
.

Let
i\in[m]
. Suppose there exists a set
\mathcal{N}_{i}\subseteq[d]
with
|\mathcal{N}_{i}|=O(1)
such that

\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}\leq O\!\left(\frac{(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right),\quad\forall j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i},

(129)

and

\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O\!\left(\frac{d(\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right).

(130)

Then, for any
\lambda\in[0.01,0.99]
,

\Pr\!\left[\left|\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\tilde{\bm{z}}^{(r)}_{n,j}+\langle\bm{w}_{i},\tilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{n}\rangle\right|\geq\lambda\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right]\lesssim e^{-\Omega(\log^{2}d)}.

(131)

Lemma E.10

(Gradient for sparse features)
.

Suppose
D.1
holds at iteration
t\geq 0
. For
j\in[d]
, we denote events

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{S_{i,t}^{\setminus j}\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}-\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}C_{\tilde{\bm{z}}}\right\},

(132)

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\bar{S}_{i,t}^{\setminus j}\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}-\bar{\alpha}_{i,j}^{(t)}C_{\tilde{\bm{z}}}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left|\bar{S}_{i,t}^{\setminus j}+\bar{\alpha}_{i,j}^{(t)}C_{\tilde{\bm{z}}}\right|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\!\left(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}C_{\tilde{\bm{z}}}-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle A_{4}

\displaystyle:=\left\{S_{i,t}^{\setminus j}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\!\left(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}C_{\tilde{\bm{z}}}-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\right\};

and quantities
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3},L_{4}
as

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\bar{S}_{i,t}^{\setminus j}|^{2}(\bm{1}_{A_{1}}+\bm{1}_{A_{2}})]}{\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\tilde{\xi}\rangle^{2}]}},\quad L_{2}:=\Pr(A_{1}),

(133)

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\bar{S}_{i,t}^{\setminus j}|^{2}(\bm{1}_{A_{3}}+\bm{1}_{A_{4}})]}{\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\tilde{\xi}\rangle^{2}]}},\quad L_{4}:=\Pr(A_{3}).

Then we have the following results:

(a) (all features)

For all
i\in[m]
, if
\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}\geq 0
, we have (when
\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}\leq 0
the opposite inequality holds)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\right]

(134)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\tfrac{\bm{z}_{X}+\bm{z}_{Y}}{2}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}+|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z}_{X}-\tfrac{\bm{z}_{X}+\bm{z}_{Y}}{2}\rangle|}\right]

\displaystyle\pm

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}+O\!\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\bar{\alpha}_{i,j}^{(t)}|^{2}}\right)\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\Big|\tfrac{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}+\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}}{2}\Big||\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}|\right]\cdot O(L_{1}+L_{2}).

(b) (lucky features)

If
\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}>\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\right]

(135)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\!\left(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}-b_{i}^{(t)}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\tfrac{\bm{z}_{X}+\bm{z}_{Y}}{2}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}+|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z}_{X}-\tfrac{\bm{z}_{X}+\bm{z}_{Y}}{2}\rangle|}\right]

\displaystyle\pm

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}+O\!\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\bar{\alpha}_{i,j}^{(t)}|^{2}}\right)\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\Big|\tfrac{\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}+\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}}{2}\Big||\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}|\right]\cdot O(L_{3}+L_{4}).

If
\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}<-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}
, then the opposite inequality holds with
(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}-\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})
replaced by
(\alpha_{i,j}^{(t)}+\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)})
.

Lemma E.11

(Gradient from dense signals)
.

Let
i\in[m]
and
j\in[d]
. Suppose
D.1
holds for the current iteration
t
. Then

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle w^{(t)}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq b^{(t)}_{i}}\langle\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]\right|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\!\left(\frac{\|w^{(t)}_{i}\|_{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\cdot\Pr\!\big(h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\neq 0\big).

(136)

For dense features
\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}
,
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
, we have a similar result:

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle w^{(t)}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq b^{(t)}_{i}}\langle\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right]\right|\leq\widetilde{O}\!\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{d\sqrt{d_{1}}}\right)\cdot\Pr\!\big(h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\neq 0\big).

(137)

E.3
Proof of Theorem E.1

Proof of Theorem
E.1
:

First we need to prove all the Theorem
E.1
hold for
t=T_{2}
. Indeed, (1), (4), (5), (6), (7) is valid at
T_{2}
from Lemma
E.9
. and Theorem
D.1
; (2) and (3) holds at
T_{2}
obviously.

Now suppose it hold for some
t\geq T_{2}
, we will prove that it still hold for
t+1
.
We first deal with the case where
j\in[d]
and
i\notin\mathcal{M}_{j}
, where it holds that

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle(1-\eta\lambda)+\eta\mathbb{E}[h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\langle\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle]

(138)

\displaystyle-\eta\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\ell_{s,t}^{\prime}\cdot h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}}\langle\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]\pm\frac{\eta}{\operatorname{poly}(d_{1})}.

In this case, to calculate the expectation, we need to use Lemma
E.10
, Lemma
E.4
.
First we compute the probability of events
A_{1}-A_{4}
by using Lemma
E.7
, Lemma
E.8
, Lemma
E.9
and our Theorem
E.1
to obtain

\Pr(A_{1}),\Pr(A_{2})\leq\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)^{\Omega(\log d)}},

(139)

which implies

L_{1},L_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)^{\Omega(\log d)}}.

(140)

Furthermore, from Fact
E.1
, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+(s)}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{(r)}\bm{1}_{|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\frac{\bm{z}_{X}^{(r)}+\bm{z}_{Y}^{(s)}}{2}\rangle|\geq\bm{b}_{i}+|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{\bm{z}_{X}}^{(r)}-\frac{\bm{z}_{X}^{(r)}+\bm{z}_{Y}^{(s)}}{2}\rangle|}\right]\leq\epsilon_{j}\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)^{\Omega(\log d)}}.

(141)

Now we further take into considerations Lemma
E.11
, Lemma
E.4
. We can obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle(1-\eta\lambda)+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\eta\Xi_{2}^{2}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\pm\frac{\eta}{\operatorname{poly}(d_{1})}.

(142)

Indeed, since we have chosen learning rate
\eta=\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}
and
\lambda\in\left[\frac{1}{d^{1.01}},\frac{1}{d^{1.49}}\right]
, it is easy to prove (5) as follows:

\bullet
For
i\notin\mathcal{M}_{j}
,
|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right)
:
This is easy since by using Lemma
E.10
, Lemma
E.4
, we can prove the following inequality by contradiction

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t-1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{\eta}{d^{2}}-\eta\lambda)+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\eta\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(143)

\displaystyle\leq\cdots\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right).

Now we begin to prove (6). For all
i\in[m]
, we have
\max_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|\leq O\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d_{1}}\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right)
at iteration
t=T_{2}
.
Now, by expanding the gradient updates of
\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle
, we can see that

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|(1-\eta\lambda)+|\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}|+|\Phi_{i,j}^{(t)}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}|

(144)

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|(1-\eta\lambda)+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{5}}{\tau\sqrt{d_{1}}d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

where the last inequality are obtained as follows: From Lemma
E.4
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}\tau}\right)\cdot\max_{i^{\prime}\in[m]}\left(|\langle w_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|\right)

(145)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}\tau}\right)\cdot\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{1}}}\right)\quad(since\max_{i^{\prime}\in[m]}|\langle w_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{1}}\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{5}}{\tau\sqrt{d_{1}}d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

After (5) and (6) are proven, it is easy to observe (1) is true at t. Below we shall prove (2), (3) and (4), after which (7) can be also trivially proven.

Indeed, (2) is a corollary of (3) and (4), since if
\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}\leq O(\tau\log^{3}d)
and (4) holds, we simply have

\displaystyle\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}+\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i},j\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}+\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle^{2}

(146)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}+O(d)\cdot O\left((\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2}\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}}{d\Xi_{2}^{10}}\right)+O(d_{1})\cdot O\left(\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}}{d_{1}\Xi_{2}^{10}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}+o\left((\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2}\frac{1}{\Xi_{2}^{10}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}\right),

which implies (2).

\displaystyle\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}+o\left((\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2}\frac{1}{\Xi_{2}^{10}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

(147)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)O(\frac{\operatorname{polylod}(d)}{d^{c}})

\displaystyle\leq O(1).

Thus we only need to prove (3) and (4). Indeed, for (3), letting
i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
, we
proceed as follows: we first write the updates of
\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle
as

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle(1-\eta\lambda)+\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}+\Phi_{i,j}^{(t)}+\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{(t)}

(148)

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle(1-\eta\lambda)+\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

where the last inequality comes again from Lemma
E.4
. Now suppose for some t we have
\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}\geq\Omega((\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}})^{2}\tau\log^{3}d)
, by Lemma
E.3
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle=

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}-\eta\lambda\right)+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(149)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\left(1+\epsilon_{j}\frac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}-\frac{\eta\lambda}{2}\right).

which means that
\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\leq\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle
. This in fact gives

\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t+1)}\leq\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}
, so that (3) is proven.

Now for (4), we need to induct as follows: for
t\leq T_{j}^{\prime}:=\frac{d\log d}{\eta\log\log d}
which is the specific iteration when
\mathfrak{F}_{j}^{(t)}\geq G_{2}\tau\log d
, where
G_{2}
is defined in Lemma
E.3
. The induction of (4) follows from similar proof in Theorem
D.1
. After
T_{j}^{\prime}
, we discuss as follows

\bullet
When
t\in\left[T_{j}^{\prime},\frac{d^{1.49}}{\eta}\right]
, from above calculations, for each
i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|}{|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|}=\frac{|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|(1-\eta\lambda)+\eta\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Xi_{2}}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|(1-\eta\lambda)+\eta\Psi_{i^{\prime},j}^{(t)}\pm\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Xi_{2}}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|_{2}}.

(150)

On one hand, for those
i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{j}\text{ such that }|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle|\leq\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\Xi_{2}^{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}\right)
, we can safely get
\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\gg\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|
. On the other hand, if
\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|\geq\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\Xi_{2}^{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}}{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle}-\frac{\Psi_{i^{\prime},j}^{(t)}}{\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle}\right|=\frac{O(\frac{\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}}{d^{2}})}{\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle}\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{2}\Xi^{2}_{2}})\leq O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{t\sqrt{d}\eta}\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}\right).

(151)

Thus by letting
\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}:=\frac{\Psi_{i,j}^{(t)}}{\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}=\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\eta\lambda)\pm O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\eta\lambda)\pm O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|_{2}}.

(152)

Since at iteration
t\in\left[T_{j}^{\prime},\frac{d^{1.49}}{\eta}\right],\text{ it is easy to obtain that }\left|\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{\eta t}\right)
.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}

(153)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1-\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}))}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1+\frac{\Xi_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}))}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1+\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1-\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}})-\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1+\frac{\Xi_{2}}{\sqrt{d}})\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}})\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\Xi^{2}_{2}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\prod_{t^{\prime}=T_{j}^{\prime}}^{t-1}\left(1-O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{t^{\prime}\sqrt{d}}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{j}^{\prime})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(T_{j}^{\prime})},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}\geq\Omega(1).

where in the last inequality we have used our Theorem
E.1
at
T_{j}^{\prime}

\bullet\quad\text{The proof for iterations }t\in\left[\frac{d^{1.49}}{\eta},\frac{d^{1.99}}{\eta}\right]
is largely similar to the above. The only difference here is that we rely on a slightly different comparison here: Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}=\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\eta\lambda)\pm O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\eta\lambda)\pm O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|_{2}}.

(154)

Here we can use similar techniques as above to require
\left|\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\Xi_{2}}{t\eta}\right)
Now we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1-\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}))}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|(1+\eta(\widetilde{\Psi}_{j}^{(t)}-\lambda)(1+\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}))}

(155)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{t\sqrt{d}}\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\prod_{t^{\prime}=d^{1.49}/\eta}^{t-1}\left(1-\frac{\Xi_{2}^{2}}{t^{\prime}d^{0.01}}\right)\cdot\frac{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(d^{1.49}/\eta)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\prime}}^{(d^{1.49}/\eta)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right|}\geq\Omega(1).

Now (4) are proven. (7) is an immediate result of our update scheme.
∎

E.4
Proof of Theorem 3.1

The first part proves the convergence of the loss function. The second part is a further extension of Theorem E.1.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

We start with the proof of convergence ((
11
) in Theorem
3.1
).

Denote
w^{(t)}=(w_{1}^{(t)},\ldots,w_{m}^{(t)})
, since our update is

w^{(t+1)}=w^{(t)}-\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})+\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{poly}(d_{1})},

(156)

we have

\displaystyle\eta\langle\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}),w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

(157)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{\eta^{2}}{2}\|\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})\|_{F}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w^{(t+1)}-\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\tfrac{\eta^{2}}{\operatorname{poly}(d_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\,\operatorname{poly}(d)+\tfrac{1}{2}\|w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w^{(t+1)}-\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\tfrac{\eta^{2}}{\operatorname{poly}(d_{1})},

where the inequality comes from

\left\|\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})\right\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})\right\|^{2}.

(158)

Each term is
O(1)
, and since
m=\operatorname{poly}(d)
, the overall complexity is
\operatorname{poly}(d)
.

Now we will use the tools from online learning to obtain a loss guarantee: define a pseudo objective for parameter
\theta

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{L}(f_{t,\theta},f_{t})+\tfrac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i\in[m]}\|\theta_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

(159)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[-\tau\log\left(\frac{e^{\langle f_{t,\theta}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\rangle/\tau}}{\sum_{\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{B}}e^{\langle f_{t,\theta}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{X})\rangle/\tau}}\right)\right]+\tfrac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i\in[m]}\|\theta_{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Which is a convex function over
\theta
since it is linear in
\theta
(for a fixed
f_{t}
, we can consider
\widetilde{L}(f_{t,\theta},f_{t})
to be convex with respect to
\theta
, because
f_{t,\theta}(x)
is linear, and softmax + log is a convex composition; the regularization term is convex).

Moreover, we have

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(w^{(t)})=L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}),

(160)

and

\nabla_{\theta_{i}}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)})=\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}).

(161)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\eta\langle\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}),w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\langle\nabla_{\theta}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(w^{(t)}),w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\textcircled{\raisebox{-0.3pt}{\tiny\ensuremath{1}}}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(w^{(t)})-\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(\theta^{\star})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(w^{(t)})-\mathbb{E}\left[-\tau\log\left(\frac{e^{\langle f_{t,\theta^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\rangle/\tau}}{\sum_{\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{B}}e^{\langle f_{t,\theta^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),f_{t}(\bm{X})\rangle/\tau}}\right)\right]-\tfrac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i\in[m]}\|\theta_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\textcircled{\raisebox{-0.3pt}{\tiny\ensuremath{2}}}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}(w^{(t)})-O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\log d}\right)-\sum_{i\in[m]}O(\lambda\|\theta_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})-O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\log d}\right).

\raisebox{-0.3pt}{\tiny\ensuremath{1}}⃝
is because the surrogate objective function
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{aug}_{t}}
is a convex function with respect to
\theta
, so we can use a first-order convex lower bound:
f(\theta)-f(\theta^{\prime})\leq\langle\nabla f(\theta),\theta-\theta^{\prime}\rangle
.
\raisebox{-0.3pt}{\tiny\ensuremath{2}}⃝
is because
\sum_{i\in[m]}\lambda\|\theta_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}}\lambda\|\theta_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}}\lambda\frac{\tau\kappa^{2}}{|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}|^{2}}=\sum_{j\in[d]}\lambda\frac{\tau\kappa^{2}}{|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}|}=\frac{\lambda\tau\kappa^{2}}{|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}|}

Now choosing
\kappa=\Theta(\Xi_{2})\leq\tfrac{1}{\lambda d}
(so that
\sum_{i\in[m]}\lambda\|\theta_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}<\tfrac{1}{\log d}
), and by a telescoping summation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=T_{3}}^{T_{3}+T-1}\left(L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t})-O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\log d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=T_{3}}^{T_{3}+T-1}\eta\langle\nabla_{w}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}),w^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}\rangle

(163)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{O(\|w^{(T_{3})}-\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2})}{T\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{O\!\left(\|w^{(T_{3})}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}-2\,\mathrm{Tr}((w^{(T_{3})})^{\top}\theta^{\star})\right)}{T\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{O\!\left(\|w^{(T_{3})}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\theta^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}\right)}{T\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{O\!\left(m\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(T_{3})}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}{T\eta}

\displaystyle\leq O\!\left(\tfrac{m\Xi_{2}}{T\eta}\right).

Since
T\eta\;\geq\;m\Xi_{2}^{10}
, this proves the claim.

For (
12
) in Theorem
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}

(164)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}O\!\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\tfrac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}\,\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right)\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}O\!\left(\tfrac{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d_{1}}\,\Xi_{2}^{5}}\right)\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}\alpha_{i,j}\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i},\,j\in[d]}\alpha^{\prime}_{i,j}\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\beta_{i,j}\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}.

From Lemma
B.2
(c), we know that for each
j\in[d]
, there is at least one neuron that can fully learn the feature
\bm{M}_{j}
, and at most
\Xi_{2}
neurons can learn the feature
\bm{M}_{j}
. Combining this with Theorem
E.1
(c):

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{2}=\Theta\!\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}\tau\log^{3}d\right),

(165)

we can conclude that the range of
\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle
is
[\tfrac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\tfrac{\tau}{\Xi_{2}},\;\tfrac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\tau]
,
and hence the range of
\alpha_{i,j}
is
[\tfrac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\frac{\tau}{\Xi_{2}},\;\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\tau]
. Furthermore, from Theorem
E.1
(e) and (f), we can obtain that
\alpha^{\prime}_{i,j}\leq o(\frac{\epsilon_{j}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
and
\beta_{i,j}\leq o(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{1}}})
respectively.

Next, we compute the upper bound of
|\mathcal{N}_{i}|
. As a first step, we calculate the expectation of
|\mathcal{N}_{i}|
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{N}_{i}|]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\mathcal{N}_{i}|=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|\mathcal{M}_{j}|\leq\frac{1}{m}\cdot d\cdot O(d^{\omega_{2}})

(166)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\cdot O(d^{1+\omega_{2}})=\frac{O(d^{1+\omega_{2}})}{d^{C_{m}}}=O\!\left(d^{1+\omega_{2}-C_{m}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\!\left(d^{1-\left(\frac{\epsilon_{\min}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}\cdot(1-\gamma)}\right).

Fix a neuron
i
, we have:
\mu_{i}:=\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{N}_{i}|
. By Bernstein’s inequality,

\ \Pr\!\left[\,|\mathcal{N}_{i}|\geq\mu_{i}+t\,\right]\;\leq\;\exp\!\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(\mu_{i}+t/3)}\right),\qquad t\geq 0.

(167)

We set
t=3\big(\sqrt{\mu_{i}L}+L\big)
and plug this into the inequality above. Then we obtain

\Pr\!\left[\,|\mathcal{N}_{i}|\;\geq\;\mu_{i}+3\big(\sqrt{\mu_{i}L}+L\big)\,\right]\;\leq\;e^{-L}.

(168)

Hence, for any constant
c>0
, taking
L=c\log d
yields

\Pr\!\left[\,|\mathcal{N}_{i}|\leq\mu_{i}+3(\sqrt{\mu_{i}c\log d}+c\log d)\,\right]\;\geq\;1-d^{-c}.

(169)

Next, we apply the union bound. For the event

A_{i}:=\Big\{\,|\mathcal{N}_{i}|\leq\mu_{i}+3(\sqrt{\mu_{i}L}+L)\,\Big\},

(170)

the union bound gives

\Pr\!\left[\bigcap_{i=1}^{m}A_{i}\right]\;\geq\;1-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Pr(A_{i}^{c})\;\geq\;1-me^{-L}.

(171)

Taking
L=c\log(md)
, we obtain

\Pr\!\left[\,\forall i\in[m],\ |\mathcal{N}_{i}|\leq\mu_{i}+3\big(\sqrt{\mu_{i}c\log(md)}+c\log(md)\big)\,\right]\;\geq\;1-(md)^{-c}.

(172)

We know
\mu_{i}\gg\log(md)
, so we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{N}_{i}|

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}\Big(1\pm O\!\big(\sqrt{\tfrac{\log(md)}{\mu_{i}}}\big)\Big)

(173)

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}\Big(1\pm o(1)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O\!\left(d^{1-\left(\frac{\epsilon_{\min}}{\epsilon_{\max}}\right)^{2}\cdot(1-\gamma)}\right)\quad\text{with probability at least }1-(md)^{-c}

Finally, for each dictionary atom
\bm{M}_{j}
, there are at least
\Omega(d^{\omega_{1}})
neurons
i\in[m]
such that
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\{j\}
. From Lemma
B.2
(c), we recall that
|\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}|\geq\Omega(d^{\omega_{1}})
. Moreover, if a neuron belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\star}
, then it cannot belong to
\mathcal{M}_{j^{\prime}}
.

For (
12
) in Theorem
3.1
, our proof is complete.
∎

Appendix F
Theorem F.1

From Lemma
B.2
(c), we know that for each
j\in[d]
, there is at least one neuron that can fully learn the minority feature
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
. When we prune out the lucky neurons that learn these minority features during the forward pass, the network will force the lucky neurons to further strengthen their feature learning ability on the minority features during the backward pass.

After magnitude pruning, neurons encoding a specific minority feature are removed. Pruning these lucky neurons reduces
\mathrm{sim}_{\bm{f_{\theta}}}(\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n})
during the forward pass. The decrease in similarity reduces the positive logit
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
, which in turn increases the gradient of the loss function, thereby encouraging these lucky neurons to further enhance their learning ability on the minority features.

Fix one specific minority feature
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
, and let
\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j^{\star}}\subseteq[m]
denote the subset of neurons primarily aligned with it, with
|\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j^{\star}}|=n
. For a pruning rate
\alpha\in[1/m,\,n/m]
, the number of pruned neurons is
\alpha m\leq n
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j^{\star}}
be the pruned set with
|\mathcal{P}|=\alpha m
.

F.1
Theorem F.1

Theorem F.1

(Feature Dynamics After Pruning)
.

Starting from the pruning stage
T_{4}
with pruning ratio
\alpha
, the following statements hold.

(a) When
i^{\star}\in\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j^{\star}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\geq\left(1-\eta\lambda+\eta\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{\alpha m\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\log\log d}{d\Xi^{2}_{2}}\right)\right)\right)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle.

(174)

(b) When
i\notin\mathcal{M}^{\star}_{j^{\star}}
and
j\neq j^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\leq\Bigg(1-\eta\lambda

\displaystyle+\eta\epsilon_{j}\,\frac{C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\bigg(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)

(175)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}\Big(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)+O\!\Big(\tfrac{\alpha m\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\log\log d}{d\Xi^{2}_{2}}\Big)\Big)\bigg)\Bigg)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle.

(c) For each neuron
i\in\mathcal{P}
and
t\in[T_{4},T_{5}]
, contrastive learning learns the following decomposition:

\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}=\alpha_{i,j^{\star}}\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}+\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}\alpha^{\prime}_{i,j}\bm{M}_{j}+\sum_{j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]}\beta_{i,j}\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp},

(176)

where

\alpha_{i,j^{\star}}\in\Bigg[\frac{\tau}{\Xi_{2}},\tau\;\Bigg],\quad\alpha^{\prime}_{i,j}\leq o\!\left(\big(1+\frac{1}{d}\big)\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2},\quad|\beta_{i,j}|\leq o\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{1}}}\right)\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}.

(177)

F.2
Useful Lemmas

Lemma F.1

(Expected values of neuron activations after
T_{4}
)
.

From
T_{4}
onward, the following results hold:

(a) For positive pair,

\mathbb{E}\!\Bigg[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}\!\big(\bm{X}_{n}\big)\,h_{i}\!\big(\bm{Y}_{n}\big)\Bigg]\;\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\alpha m\,\frac{\tau^{2}}{\Xi_{2}^{2}}\,\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right).

(178)

(b) For negative pair,

\mathbb{E}\!\Bigg[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}\!\big(\bm{X}_{n}\big)\,h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\Bigg]\;=\;0.

(179)

(c) For negative pair,

\mathbb{E}\Big[h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\,\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]\;=\;0.

(180)

Lemma F.2

(Effect of Pruning on Positive Logit Weight)
.

At the pruning stage, for the data following distribution
D_{1}
, the post-pruning positive logit
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}\!\left[1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}\right]\geq\Theta\!\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi_{2}^{2}}\,\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right).

(181)

Lemma F.3

(Positive gradient)
.

Let
h_{i,t}(\cdot)
denote the
i
-th neuron at iteration
t\leq T_{1}
(so that
\bm{b}_{i}^{(t)}=0
). Then the following hold:

(a) For each
j\in[d]
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]=\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right].

(182)

(b) For each
j\in[d_{1}]\setminus[d]
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[h_{i,t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right]=0.

(183)

F.3
Proof of Theorem F.1

Overview of the proof: first, the data can be divided into two parts: the samples that contain
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
and those that do not. The former follow distribution
D_{1}
, while the latter follow distribution
D_{2}
. Next, let us examine
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
. The values of
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
differ depending on the distribution: for samples from
D_{1}
, we have
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}=1-\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau})-\Omega(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d})
, whereas for samples from
D_{2}
,
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}=1-\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau})
. Since the latter do not contain
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
, pruning does not affect them.

Proof of Theorem F.1.

For any neuron
i^{\star}\in\mathcal{P}
we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

(184)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle-\eta\,\big\langle\nabla_{w_{i^{\star}}}L_{\mathrm{aug}}(f_{t}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\big\rangle\pm\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}}\!\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}(\bm{X}_{n},\mathfrak{B}))\cdot h_{{i^{\star}},t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}}h_{i^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\eta\sum_{\bm{X}_{n,s}\in\mathfrak{N}}\mathbb{E}\!\Big[l^{\prime}_{s,t}(\bm{X}_{n},\mathfrak{B})\,h_{{i^{\star}},t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}}h_{i^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]\pm\frac{\|\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)}\|_{2}}{\operatorname{poly}(d)}

At stage
T_{4}
, pruning is applied. We regard
\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{s,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}
as fixed, and by combining Lemma
F.1
(c) with the law of total probability, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

(185)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}}\Big[h_{{i^{\star}},t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}}h_{i^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[h_{{i^{\star}},t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}}h_{i^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[h_{{i^{\star}},t}(\bm{Y}_{n})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}}h_{i^{\star}}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}

Combining Lemma
F.3
(a) with (
181
) in Lemma
F.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

(186)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}\right]\rangle\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}\right]\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\left(\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi_{2}^{2}}\,\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right)\right)\cdot\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\cdot\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\cdot\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\cdot 0\cdot 1\cdot\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle+\eta\left(\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi_{2}^{2}}\,\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right)\right)\cdot\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\cdot\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}

Hence, the post-pruning one-step update along
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
is

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\eta\lambda+\eta\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\left(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{\alpha m\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\log\log d}{d\Xi^{2}_{2}}\right)\right)\right)\langle\bm{w}_{i^{\star}}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle.

(187)

Similarly to (
186
), for any neuron
i\notin\mathcal{P}
, we have:

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

(188)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle+\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}\Big[\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right]\rangle\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{1}}

\displaystyle+\eta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}})\Big]\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}\Big[\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right]\Big]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}\sim D_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\eta\lambda)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle+\eta\left(\Theta\!\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)+\Omega\!\left(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi_{2}^{2}}\,\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\,\frac{\log\log d}{d}\right)\right)\cdot\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\cdot\epsilon_{j}\frac{\log\log d}{d}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}

\displaystyle+\eta\cdot\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\cdot\epsilon_{j}\frac{\log\log d}{d}\cdot 1\cdot\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle

Hence, the post-pruning one-step update along
M_{j}
is

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t+1)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\leq\bigg(1

\displaystyle-\eta\lambda+\eta\epsilon_{j}\,\frac{C_{z}\log\log d}{d}\Big(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\right)

(189)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}\Big(\Theta\!\left(\tfrac{1}{\operatorname{polylog}(d)}\Big)+O\!\left(\tfrac{\alpha m\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\log\log d}{d\Xi^{2}_{2}}\right)\right)\Big)\bigg)\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle.

The above constitutes the proof of Theorem F.1 regarding pruning.
∎

F.4
Proof of Theorem 3.2

Theorem
3.2
(a) and (b) can be derived as simplifications of Theorem
F.1
(a) and (b). Theorem
3.2
(c) coincides with Theorem
F.1
(c). By taking the elapsed time
T=\frac{((\epsilon_{\max}/\epsilon_{j^{\star}})-1)d}{\eta\alpha\epsilon_{j^{\star}}^{2}C_{z}\log\log d}
and simplifying (a) and (b), then substituting into the conclusion of Theorem 3.1, the proof follows.

F.5
Proof of Lemma
F.1
:

Proof of Lemma
F.1
:

The alignment with the target minority feature
\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}
is
\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle
, and we have
|\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle|\geq\Omega(\frac{\tau}{\Xi_{2}})
at
T_{4}
(This is the conclusion of Theorem
3.1
, which can be found in the second part of the proof of Theorem
3.1
. For the positive pair
(\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n})
, the latent variables
\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}}
and
\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+}
are correlated through the augmentation process. For a negative sample
\bm{X}_{n,s}
, its latent variable
\bm{z}_{n,s,j^{\star}}
is independent of those of the positive pair
(\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}},\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+})
, so we have:

(\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}},\bm{z}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+})\perp\!\!\!\perp\bm{z}_{n,s,j^{\star}}.

(190)

For the anchor
\bm{X}_{n}
and its positive
\bm{Y}_{n}
, we have

h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})=\sum_{r=1}^{L}\Big\langle\bm{w}_{i},z^{(r)}_{Y}\Big\rangle=\frac{1}{L}\Big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n}^{(r)}+\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)}\Big\rangle,

(191)

h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\;=\sum_{s=1}^{L}\Big\langle\bm{w}_{i},z^{(s)}_{Y}\Big\rangle=\frac{1}{L}\Big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}+\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{+(s)}\Big\rangle,

(192)

\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}:=\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\bm{z}}_{n}^{(r)},\qquad\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}:=\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)},\qquad\hat{\xi}_{n}:=\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)},\qquad\hat{\xi}_{n}^{+}:=\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{+(s)}.

(193)

We can write the outputs as:

h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})=\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}+\hat{\xi}_{n}\big\rangle,\qquad h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})=\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}+\hat{\xi}_{n}^{+}\big\rangle.

(194)

For a negative sample
\bm{X}_{n,s}
:

\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}:=\sum_{q=1}^{L}\tilde{z}_{n,s}^{(q)},\quad\hat{\xi}_{n,s}:=\sum_{q=1}^{L}\tilde{\xi}_{n,s}^{(q)},

the output is:

h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})=\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}+\hat{\xi}_{n,s}\big\rangle.

(195)

We first establish a lower bound for
\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})]
.

Expanding and using zero-mean and independence of latent variables and noises, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\big[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}\rangle\,\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\big]

(196)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle^{\,2}\;\mathbb{E}\!\big[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\,\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle^{\,2}\;\mathbb{E}\!\big[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}\,\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}^{+}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\frac{\tau^{2}}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}).

Therefore

\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})]\;\geq\;\Omega(\frac{\tau^{2}}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}).

(197)

Next, we compute the expectation of
h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})
.
h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})\,h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s}),

\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})]=\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\big(\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}+\hat{\xi}_{n}\big\rangle\big)\big(\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}+\hat{\xi}_{n,s}\big\rangle\big)\Big].

(198)

By the assumption, the latent variables of
\bm{X}_{n}
are independent of those of the negative
\bm{X}_{n,s}
, and all noises are mean-zero and independent. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\!\big[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}\rangle\,\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}\rangle\big]=0,\qquad\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\hat{\xi}_{n}\rangle\,\langle\bm{w}_{i},\hat{\xi}_{n,s}\rangle]=0

(199)

Therefore, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})]=0

(200)

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the pruned set with
|\mathcal{P}|=\alpha m
. Summing the per-neuron bounds over
i\in\mathcal{P}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\Big]\geq\Omega(\alpha m\frac{\tau^{2}}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}p_{j^{\star}}),

(201)

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\Big]=0.

(202)

This completes the proof of Lemma
F.1
(a)(b).

Finally, we compute the expectation of
h_{i,t},(\bm{X}_{n,s}),\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]

(203)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}+\hat{\xi}_{n,s}\big\rangle\big)\big(\frac{1}{L}\big\langle\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}+\hat{\xi}_{n},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\big\rangle\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\frac{1}{L^{2}}\big\langle\bm{w}_{i},\;\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}+\hat{\xi}_{n,s}\big\rangle\big)\big(\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}+\big\langle\hat{\xi}_{n},\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\big\rangle\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,s}\rangle\cdot\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\star}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

This completes the proof of Lemma
F.1
(c),

\mathbb{E}\Big[h_{i,t}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\,\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j^{\star}}\rangle\Big]=0.

(204)

∎

F.6
Proof of Lemma
F.2
:

Proof of Lemma
F.2
:

We link the logit to the pruning ratio and plug it into the gradient growth. Recall the softmax weights and partial derivatives

\ell^{\prime}_{p}=\frac{e^{u_{p}/\tau}}{e^{u_{p}/\tau}+\sum_{s=1}^{S}e^{u_{s}/\tau}},\qquad\ell^{\prime}_{s}=\frac{e^{u_{s}/\tau}}{e^{u_{p}/\tau}+\sum_{s=1}^{S}e^{u_{s}/\tau}},\qquad\sum_{s=1}^{S}\ell^{\prime}_{s}=1-\ell^{\prime}_{p},

(205)

u_{p}=\mathrm{Sim}_{f}(\bm{X}_{n},\bm{Y}_{n}),\quad u_{s}=\mathrm{Sim}_{f}(\bm{X}_{n},\bm{X}_{n,s}),

(206)

\frac{\partial\ell^{\prime}_{p}}{\partial u_{p}}=\frac{1}{\tau}\,\ell^{\prime}_{p}(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p}),\qquad\frac{\partial\ell^{\prime}_{p}}{\partial u_{s}}=-\frac{1}{\tau}\,\ell^{\prime}_{p}\,\ell^{\prime}_{s}.

(207)

Pruning the size
\alpha m
changes the similarities by

\Delta u_{p}=-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n}),\qquad\Delta u_{s}=-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s}).

(208)

Next, calculate the first order change of
\ell^{\prime}_{p}
, we know:

\bm{u}=(u_{p},u_{1},\dots,u_{S}),\qquad\Delta\bm{u}=(\Delta u_{p},\Delta u_{1},\dots,\Delta u_{S}).

(209)

Using multivariate Taylor expansion up to second order with remainder:

\ell_{p}^{\prime}(\bm{u}+\Delta\bm{u})-\ell_{p}^{\prime}(\bm{u})=\nabla\ell_{p}^{\prime}(\bm{u})^{\top}\Delta\bm{u}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta\bm{u}^{\top}H_{p}(\bm{u})\Delta\bm{u}+o(\|\Delta\bm{u}\|^{2}),\Delta\bm{u}\to 0.

(210)

By a first order Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\ell^{\prime}_{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\ell^{\prime}_{p}}{\partial u_{p}}\,\Delta u_{p}+\sum_{s=1}^{S}\frac{\partial\ell^{\prime}_{p}}{\partial u_{s}}\,\Delta u_{s}+o\left(\|\Delta\bm{u}\|\right)

(211)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\,\ell^{\prime}_{p}(1-\ell^{\prime}_{p})\Delta u_{p}-\frac{1}{\tau}\,\ell^{\prime}_{p}\,\sum_{s=1}^{S}\ell^{\prime}_{s}\Delta u_{s}.

We note that at
T_{4}
, by the convergence of the loss function, we obtain
\ell^{\prime}_{p}=1-\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau})
, and both
\ell^{\prime}_{p}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{s}
take fixed values. Then, by taking expectations over
\Delta\ell^{\prime}_{p}
and using the relation
\sum_{s}\ell^{\prime}_{s}=1-\ell^{\prime}_{p},
we obtain:

\mathbb{E}[\Delta\ell^{\prime}_{p}]=-\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau^{2}})\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\big]\Big).

(212)

Also, by Lemma
F.1
, given that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n})h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n,s})\Big]\geq\Omega(\alpha m\frac{\tau^{2}}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}).

(213)

Hence,

\mathbb{E}[\Delta\ell^{\prime}_{p}]=-\Omega(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d})<0.

(214)

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}}]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta\ell_{p}^{\prime}]

(215)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}}]-\Omega(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d})

\displaystyle=1-\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau})-\Omega(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}).

Now, converting to the form of
1-\ell^{\prime}
:

\mathbb{E}[1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}]=1-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}].

(216)

Substituting the previous expression gives

\mathbb{E}[1-\ell^{\prime}_{p,\bm{\theta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{mask}}}]=\Theta(\frac{1}{\tau})+\Omega(\frac{\alpha m}{\Xi^{2}_{2}}\epsilon_{j^{\star}}\frac{\log\log d}{d}).

(217)

∎

F.7
Proof of Lemma
F.3
(a):

Proof of Lemma
F.3
(a):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]

(218)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\langle\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}^{(r)}_{\left|\left\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)}\right\rangle\right|\geq 0}\cdot\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{z_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{+(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{(r)}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\left(\langle M\hat{\bm{z}}_{n},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\langle\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d]}\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime}},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d]}\langle\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime\prime}}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime\prime}}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right].

In the final step, we have

\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\in[d]}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime\prime}}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right]=\frac{1}{L^{2}}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}_{j}\rangle\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}^{+}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j}\right].

(219)

This completes the proof.
∎

F.8
Proof of Lemma
F.3
(b):

Proof of Lemma
F.3
(b):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}h_{i}(\bm{X}_{n}),\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right]

(220)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\langle\sum_{r=1}^{L}\bm{1}^{(r)}_{\left|\left\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)}\right\rangle\right|\geq 0}\cdot\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h_{i}(\bm{Y}_{n})\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{1}^{(r)}_{\left|\left\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)}\right\rangle\right|\geq 0}\cdot\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{z_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle\bm{1}^{(s)}_{\left|\left\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},z_{X}^{(s)}\right\rangle\right|\geq 0}\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{1}^{(r)}_{\left|\left\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{(t)},\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)}\right\rangle\right|\geq 0}\cdot\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{z_{Y}}^{(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{z_{X}}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{+(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{(r)}+\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{L}\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{L}\langle\bm{M}\tilde{z}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\sum_{s=1}^{L}\tilde{z}_{n}^{+(s)}\rangle\cdot\left(\langle\bm{M}\sum_{r=1}^{L}\tilde{z}_{n}^{(r)},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\left(\langle\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\langle\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d]}\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime}},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\langle\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d]}\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[d]}\langle\bm{M}_{j^{\prime}},\bm{M}_{j}^{\perp}\rangle\hat{\bm{z}}_{n,j^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{w}_{i},\bm{M}\hat{\bm{z}}_{n}^{+}\rangle\cdot 0\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

∎

Appendix G
Proof of Lemmas in Appendix B

G.1
Proof of Lemma
B.2
(a):

Proof of Lemma
B.2
(a):

At initialization, the neuron weight
\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}
is a high dimensional Gaussian vector :

\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}I_{d_{1}}),

(221)

with
\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
and each coordinate
\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}(k)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{0}^{2})
, i.i.d.

\left\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{d_{1}}\left(\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}(k)\right)^{2}.

(222)

We know that
\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}(k)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{0}^{2})
, so:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{0}^{2}}\left\|\bm{w}_{i}^{(0)}\right\|_{2}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(d_{1}).

(223)

According to the concentration inequality of the chi-square distribution:

If
X\sim\chi^{2}(d_{1})
, then for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, we have:
